# VOLUME 14 IS WRITTEN TO 690 AND THE VOLUME IS NOT CLOSED

## Volume 14 — *THE COPY*, chapters 651–700. THE FIRST OF FEBRUARY TO THE THIRTYTH OF DECEMBER, YEAR SEVEN. **FIFTY CHAPTERS AND ONE HUNDRED AND SIXTEEN THOUSAND FOUR HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-ONE WORDS ON DISK ACROSS FIVE BATCHES: 651–660 (22,791 in batch 0001), 661–670 (24,043 in batch 0002, after its repair), 671–680 (23,617 in batch 0003, re-measured after two repairs), 681–690 (22,925 in batch 0004) and 691–700 (23,095 in batch 0005, re-measured after the review-fix pass). CHAPTERS 691–700 ARE WRITTEN AND CHAPTER 700 IS THE LAST CHAPTER OF THE VOLUME.** `outline/volume-14.md` CARRIES THE SENTENCE, THE SPINE, THE SCENE SCHEDULE, THE GATES AND THE TITLE BANK, AND **ALL FIVE OF ITS §7 MOVEMENTS ARE NOW WRITTEN AGAINST THE PAGE**. `state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED AND **WHETHER IT IS CREATED IS NOT A DECISION THE PHASE THAT WROTE 691–700 MADE**. `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED IN EVERY PASS. **NO NEXT PHASE EXISTS AND NONE WAS CREATED.**

**WHERE THE VOLUME STANDS AT 700, IN ONE PARAGRAPH.** The head of the standing sheet is at **eight hundred and fifty-two** and has not moved once in fifty chapters, and the twenty-eighth sheet went up at four on the thirtieth of December unmoved across twelve of them. The second column went from two lines to three on the twelfth of September and the entry is **a receipt and not the claims**. Four hundred and nine pieces of pencil paper are in two cardboard boxes on the floor of a room nobody is obliged to open, on the written instruction of the only man entitled to give one, which is dated and signed and cannot be taken back. **The index that could correct them is in nine or ten hands and is not in the office, and one of those copies has nine people's corrections in the margin and a page and a half more than the original.** The shutter has been bare for four hundred and eleven days and about two thousand people a day walk past a pale rectangle. The wall is nine feet of new render with four inches of dressed stone at the bottom of it and about two hundred people a day at the corner, and a boy with a paste brush has been putting a month's form on a board there sixty-four times and nobody has ever asked him whether it is the right thing to be doing. **A bundle of twenty-five things went into the drawer on the last working day of the year and he shut it with his own hand, and the only piece of paper in this city that says the two words were cut before the stone was delivered is in the inside pocket of a rank-one officer and he has not read the date on it.** **And the sentence at the foot of the most widely read page in this city — where the Register has nothing, it has not been given anything — stopped being true of the only building in Ostara that prints it, at ten past eleven on the thirtieth of August, and there is no form anywhere on which a building may print a correction about itself.**

**THE SPINE, RE-DERIVED TWICE BY METHODS THAT SHARE NO CONSTANT, ZERO MONOTONIC VIOLATIONS ACROSS EVERY CHAPTER WRITTEN.** `651 = 2,160 = 6 × 360 exactly`, the first day the whole calendar divides by, and `2,160 − 2,129 = 31` days after chapter 650. `690 = 2,420`. Quarter-ends written at **660 (2,219)**, **673 (2,309)**, **687 (2,399)** and **700 (2,489)**, all four, which is the whole of them, because this volume opened on the first of February and the thirty-first of January is not a day in this city. Sheet ordinals **twenty-fifth, twenty-sixth, twenty-seventh, twenty-eighth**; the head stands at eight hundred and fifty-two unmoved across **twelve** sheets on the continuity the chapters themselves print, and `outline/volume-14.md` §5b's own head row says eight and nine and is **flagged and not corrected**, §5b being one of the seven sections a batch may not touch. Batch rows: `7 7 6 7 6 7 6 7 6 = 59` and `7 7 7 7 7 7 7 7 7 = 63` and `7 6 7 6 7 6 7 6 7 = 59` and `7 6 6 6 6 6 7 7 7 = 58` and `7 7 7 7 7 7 7 7 6 = 62`, plus a seam of 31 and four seams of 7 — **and three of those four rows were printed wrong in a phase prompt and repaired against the page in the state file rather than in the chapter.**

**THE SCENES WRITTEN ARE 653, 663, 673 AND 683, CADENCE 10 · 10 · 10 · 10.** Per-woman, reported and never certified: **Beatrix Halm 79** (last scene 574) · **Agnete Lindqvist 99** (564) · **Hanneke Ruis 119** (554, the largest per-woman claim in the book since Sun Meiling's five hundred and fifty at 613, and the first scene in this book where the woman says no on the page, gives the reason out loud, and the scene happens anyway) · **Tessa Okonkwo 89** (594). **The fifth is owed at 693 — Ines Duarte, fifty chapters after her own — and is not written.** **All four written axes are new to this manuscript: the two readings, the stand, the shelf, the chart.** None is one of Volume 12's five and none is one of Volume 13's five, and §4d's borrowed-body and borrowed-staging table was read before a body was planned in every one of them, which is how the lift of Agnete Lindqvist's staging out of 663 into 673 and the lift of three pieces of Tessa Okonkwo's own furniture out of 594 and 645 into 683 were both caught and rewritten in the chapters.

**WHAT VOLUME 14 REFUSES, AND EACH ONE IS A WAY THIS MANUSCRIPT HAS ALREADY SPENT ITSELF:** it does not reopen 500 and does not re-argue the reach of the return · it is not a climb and he is rank one throughout and nothing moves his hours, his plate, his quarters or his standing · it is not a second definition and not a second census · **it introduces no new enemy** · it does not fill the two inches with anything the office can reach · it does not fill either repainting box · it does not print the figure of the account or any floor or any running balance in grains · it names no day of the week and no year numeral in prose and no announced age · **it does not print the invented-figures sentence verbatim anywhere but 658, and does not use the near-verbatim anywhere in the volume after it** · it does not land on or name or reach across the eighth or the tenth of October · it does not reopen Hanneke Ruis's six words or spend her one question again · it does not answer Ines Duarte's four questions · it does not say whether the woman two flights up ever learned, **because that item was closed at 615 and 617 and stays closed** · it does not thank Brack, defeat him, or make him wrong · it does not spend the counterman's one question twice · **and it introduced Perla Quist, who is not an enemy, is not the woman at the corner who cannot write her own name, and by 680 has the only index in Ostara and will not hand it to anybody, including the only man entitled to ask her.**

## Volume 13, Batch 0005 — Chapters 641–650. THE THIRTIETH OF OCTOBER TO THE THIRTIETH OF DECEMBER, YEAR SIX. **32,294 WORDS ACROSS TEN FILES, COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND, RE-MEASURED FROM DISK BY THE 14 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW-FIX. MOVEMENT FIVE, *PAID*. THE SCENE AT 643 IS INES DUARTE'S — 59 CHAPTERS AFTER HER OWN LAST ONE AND 10 CHAPTERS AFTER THE SCENE AT 633 — AND IT IS IN AN AUDIT ROOM ON THE TABLE THAT HAS HAD NOTHING ON IT SINCE THE FIRST OF OCTOBER OF THE THIRD YEAR. THE VOLUME'S LAST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 644 AND IT IS HIS AND IT IS A PEN. 648 BRINGS SIXTY-TWO BLANK FORMS AND THE FIRST RUBBING EVER MADE ON THAT WALL OUT FROM UNDER SIX YEARS OF PAINT. 650 IS THE TWENTY-FOURTH SHEET AT FOUR AND A BOY WHO SAYS HE WILL BE PAINTING THAT CORNER IN THE SPRING. `outline/batches/volume-13-batch-0005.md` AND `outline/volume-13.md` §7 MOVEMENT FIVE ARE WRITTEN AGAINST THE PAGE IN THIS COMMIT. THE ONE NEXT PHASE IS A VOLUME CLOSE FOR VOLUME 13, WHICH IS A SEPARATE PHASE AND IS NOT WRITTEN HERE. `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED AND `state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED.**

| **641** | 30 Oct, y6 · 2,069 · 2,069 − 2,060 = **9** after 640 | **A Chart With A Line Going Down** | **2,998** | **THE CHART, AND IT IS A CHART OF A THING FINISHING.** He is doing the main stair in **four** where he did it in two until the twenty-third and the woman who has been counting his landings since the middle of October says so before he can. **A RULED BOARD IN A DRAWER: ELEVEN COLUMNS, FOUR HEADED WITH NAMES, FOUR RULED EMPTY, A HEAVY LINE ACROSS THE FOOT OF EVERY COLUMN AND NOTHING BELOW ANY OF THEM.** A fortieth is the smallest share she can lift, it goes into her and does not stay behind in the person, and it has never once been a store. **THE COLUMN WITH HIS PLATE AGAINST THE HEAD OF IT HAS ELEVEN ENTRIES AND THE MARKS HAVE GOT SMALLER AND THE LINE ONLY GOES DOWN, and there has been no line on that board for going up since the first of February of the first year.** **She breaks the eleven-year rule of the ward — she takes a share and does not tell, and they do not ask — and she breaks it for him, and says why before she does it: he is not a woman who works nights and he is not on her rota, and she has waited nine days for him to ask and he did not, and a want would have done it and he had a need.** He asks whether it can be put back and the answer is no, and the reason is that he would learn to lean on a place and in about eleven weeks he would be back to the spring with eleven weeks less than that. **A woman on the fourth floor of that building who asked, in the fourth year, to be put on the board so that her sister would be told a thing, and was refused, and is on that ward now and does not know why she is here.** Want: somebody to put it back. Emotion: the plain fear of a man discovering he is a resource and not a person. Ends: eleven marks and a bucket and a stair he did in three. |
| **642** | 7 Nov, y6 · 2,076 · 2,076 − 2,069 = **7** | **Four Names On A Folded Page** | **2,902** | **THE SEAM, AND AN ENSEMBLE CHAPTER AND NOT A SCENE.** The rota page about the size of a folded hand, four names and the same four for six years and a fifth line never filled, because a page about hours is a page about hours and not about anybody. **THE COPY OF THE STANDING SHEET HAS BEEN LYING FACE UP ON THE COUNTER BY THE NAIL FOR EIGHT NIGHTS**, because the bundle came up an hour late on the thirtieth and the man who carries it had to sign on the counter and put it down to do it and then went away, and not one person has turned it over since. **NADIA FERROUS HAS READ IT EVERY NIGHT SINCE AND WILL NOT TURN IT FACE DOWN**, because the next person off that stair would then not read it, and she is not going to be the reason. Petra Ilyin explains, in one sentence, why she has not read it on the other five nights. **ISOLDE MARCHETTI, WHO HAS READ THE SAME PAPER AT THE SAME COUNTER FOR ABOUT NINE HUNDRED NIGHTS AND HAS NEVER SAID WHAT, SAYS IT ONCE AND ONLY ONCE: it is the only writing she has ever had in her head that is worth the tune, and it is a bad tune, and three of the four sentences at the foot of it are about how to use a piece of paper.** At half past one **INES DUARTE COMES DOWN THE CORRIDOR AND NADIA PUTS THE QUESTION TO HER — is it still true — AND SHE DECLINES TO ANSWER IT**, because a sentence said in a corridor at half past one is a sentence a corridor has heard and a corridor has four names on it. **She will read the whole page aloud to one person, in order, in a room with a shut door in it, on a night she picks, and she will not warn him, and the reason she gives is that if she warns him then the two people who matter will prepare for it.** Want: somebody to be angry about that page, and the woman at the counter says he has never once been angry about it in two years and she would like to know why. Ends: the fifteenth of November, and rain for the first time since the middle of the ninth. |
| **643** | 15 Nov, y6 · 2,084 · 2,084 − 2,076 = **8** · 643 − 633 = **10** · 643 − 584 = **59** · **SCENE** | **The Question He Has Carried Since March** | **4,383** | **THE SCENE. INES DUARTE, 59 after 584, 10 after 633.** An audit room, two tables, one bracket lamp, ninety feet of corridor. **HE SITS ON THE FAR TABLE, WHICH HAS HAD NOTHING ON IT SINCE THE FIRST OF OCTOBER OF THE THIRD YEAR AND IS THE ONLY SURFACE IN THE ROOM THAT HAS NEVER HAD A SHEET PUT ON IT AND READ OFF, AND THE SHEET STAYS ON THE NEAR TABLE UNDER THE LAMP** — which is the whole of the reason, and it is a reason and not a mood, and in 584 she turned the page face down to get to him. **THE AXIS BROKEN IS THE HOUR**, carried by the room's rule and named by nobody: *the date first, the hour with it, nothing skipped*, eleven words, and an auditor does not stop to be spoken to while a page is open. **AND IN THIS CHAPTER SHE DECLINES TO GIVE AN HOUR.** He has carried a question since the seventh of March and it is a question about a man and not about the sentence, and she says so, and gives him the page instead. **SHE READS IT IN ORDER** — the head, about four hundred and nineteen lines with a date and an hour against every hour and no figure, the second column with two lines, the clerk's sentence under his own entry, and the four sentences at the foot, **his own two given in her mouth in other words and not as written**. **HE ASKS FOR THE HOUR ON THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER, WHICH IS THE ONLY FIGURE ON THAT PAGE HE HAS EVER BEEN ABLE TO MAKE A CLAIM OUT OF. SHE SAYS NO**, and the reason is that every hour she has certified in eleven years sits underneath a sentence saying that any number a person sets down in this city may be one they invented on purpose. **AND THEN SHE GIVES HIM ONE, AND IT IS FOUR O'CLOCK, AND IT IS THE ONLY HOUR IN OSTARA THAT NOBODY ARGUES ABOUT, AND IT IS NOT THE ONE HE CAME FOR.** **HIS FOUR QUESTIONS — three on the sixth of March of the first year and a fourth at a door on the fourteenth of September of the fifth — ARE NOT ANSWERED HERE, AND SAYING SO IS ALL SHE DOES WITH THEM.** **THE SCENE IS PERFORMED INSIDE THE SCARCITY: she does not stop reading, and she reads him from the top in order with the date and the hour against every line, and the fourth line is the only one not in the record — you are about a fortieth less than you were and there is nothing behind that, and there is not going to be anything behind that, and how I know is that I have been in this room four nights in seven for three years and I read you across forty feet every night without ever having to come near you.** He is rank one with a spent chest and no permission; he is measurably less than he was and she is entitled to know that and knows it. **A page turns once, at about the halfway point, and she turns it with her thumb and does not look down, because it is the next line in the order.** Afterwards she goes back to the sheet, squares it, finds her place with her thumb and carries on, **and does not turn it face down**, which is what she did on the fourteenth of September of the fifth year and what it cost her. **Closes on the thing she has done once a year in the eleventh month for about nine years, in a room with a shut door in it, which is not an office and is not in any book and which she is not going to tell anybody about.** Set B at word boundary: **0**; 603 returns 1, 623 and 633 return 0. |
| **644** | 23 Nov, y6 · 2,092 · 2,092 − 2,084 = **8** | **He Strikes His Own Line** | **3,237** | **THE VOLUME'S LAST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS HIS.** Seven days to the thirtieth and a page with a hole about to go out in it. **HE ASKS FOR THE FOOT OF THE SHEET AND WRITES THE FOUR CONDITIONS OUT UNDER THE FOUR SENTENCES, AS HE ENTERED THEM ON THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER OF THE FOURTH YEAR, IN HIS OWN HAND, IN THE HAND OF THE OFFICE. HE TAKES THE FOURTH — *and it is not on a page anywhere in this city* — FROM THE FIRST LETTER TO THE LAST WITH ONE STROKE, WRITES HIS NAME AND THE DATE UNDER IT, AND WRITES NOTHING IN THE TWO INCHES WHERE IT WAS.** **They go at the foot and not in the column, because a column takes an entry and an entry goes in, and the foot is the only part of a sheet anybody in this city reads to the bottom for, and there are about nine people a week.** **WENNA RAUT SPENDS THE ONE QUESTION SHE HAS THAT MORNING AND IT IS WHETHER THE SIXTH LINE IS A LINE OR A SECOND PARAGRAPH**, because a clerk enters the two differently and about nine people a week will be able to tell on the first of January. She holds no pen and is not asked to. **WHAT THE FOURTH LINE WAS CARRYING FOR SIX YEARS WAS THE CLAIMS OF PEOPLE WHO CANNOT READ**: a claim wants a page, and a person who cannot read a lease, a demand, a finding or a sheet has never been able to put one of anything anywhere. **A woman off Lamplit Row who cannot read a lease she signed and has known she was short every month for nine years can now say almost anything at all. She cannot say that anybody took anything off her.** Yusuf Adeyemi gives four things that are true and none of them is comfort, and one of them is that **an entry can be made and an entry cannot be taken back, because the only instrument that could take one back is a person being wrong about a thing that is already written down, and this city has never built one of those and he has looked.** He is not thanked, is not told he was right, gets no replacement condition, and is forgiven by nobody. Ends at half past four: the shutter has been bare since the ninth of November, about nine hundred people took the other three off in about nine hours without asking anybody, the woman of about sixty says she did not put a notice up, **and a man with a child on his shoulders writes *that is worse* on the back of his hand in chalk and walks up the path without looking back.** |

What she could take now is ordinary and she could give it back in nine minutes and will not, because giving back is not what the board is for. Nine minutes of the worst night of his working year, **and it is not a page and he would not have written it down.** **SHE TELLS HIM ONCE, AND ONCE ONLY, THAT HE WILL BE SLOWER THAN HE WAS IN THE SPRING FOR THE REST OF HIS WORKING LIFE, THAT NOBODY IN THIS CITY CAN PUT IT BACK, THAT SHE HAS LOOKED, AND THAT LOOKING IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT SHE IS FOR.** Four hundred days priced: a rota, three floors of washrooms, a bin room, a bucket, a hill in the eighth month for nine days, and two flights of stairs on the fourth of March with nobody at the top of them. **Ends at the foot of a wall in the dark, and the whole of the spring arrives in about nine seconds: the render comes off, the wall goes down to the course, and the bottom course is dressed stone and does not come off, so the four inches will still be there.** |
| **646** | 7 Dec, y6 · 2,106 · 2,106 − 2,099 = **7** | **A Figure She Kept Nine Years** | **2,819** | **THE YARD WITH NOTHING ON IT.** About eleven thousand people, about nine hundred of them able to read all of it, and **about two thousand a day slowing down at the foot of a shutter that has had nothing at the bottom of it since the ninth of November.** **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY HAS TOLD ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND NINETY PEOPLE THAT A NUMBER MEANT ONE THING, AND HAS BEEN SAYING IT INTO AN EMPTY WALL FOR ABOUT TWENTY-EIGHT DAYS, AND IT GETS EASIER EVERY DAY, AND THAT IS THE PART SHE CANNOT STAND.** She says she did not put a notice up and that in about two years there is going to be somebody who says a woman of about sixty took them down. **AGNETE LINDQVIST IS ON THE STEPS AND ASKS HOW MANY PEOPLE ARE IN THE YARD THIS MORNING**, waits about four seconds, and says: *I have said four hundred on these steps once a month for about sixteen months and I have never once been answered, and it is not four hundred, and I cannot work out what it is, and neither can anybody else who says it.* He does not go over. **THE CHAPTER'S SPINE IS FIGURES NOBODY WRITES DOWN:** ninety-one people a quarter on a woman's fingers for four years and then given up; three questions in a day book that went to nobody; thirty names in a pencil ledger a man started keeping in the autumn because somebody set a paper down two inches from his elbow. **AND THE FIGURE IN NUMBER FOUR OFF LAMPLIT ROW, WHO CANNOT READ, WHO ADDS UP WHAT SHE IS SHORT BY ON HER FINGERS AT THE END OF EVERY MONTH AND HAS BEEN SHORT EVERY MONTH FOR NINE YEARS.** She puts the demand of the twentieth of July on the counter and turns the address to the head of it and says she has not come to have anything written down, and that if she is put on a counter with a box she will go home and not come again. **HE DOES NOT WRITE IT DOWN.** She makes him say it out loud, because she cannot read it and has never heard it, **and then she says it back after him, badly, with the middle of it in the wrong place, and he does not correct her.** Wants: to be a person somebody has said a figure to. I can write a thing down. That is the whole of what I have.*** **And he asks whether the fourth line was ever on a board anywhere, and is told: not one that anybody put there after the seventh of July, and none at all after today.** Want: somebody to tell him he could not do anything. | **648** | 22 Dec, y6 · 2,121 · 2,121 − 2,114 = **7** | **Stone Under The Paint** | **3,194** | **THE WALL, OPENED AT 639 AND 640, AND WHAT IS UNDER SIX YEARS OF MONTHLY PAINT.** The boy at the kerb with a board under his arm and a quarter of an hour of not doing it. **HE ASKS FOR THE PAINT TO BE TAKEN OFF — a hand's width above the four inches, in front, in two hours, before four o'clock — AND IT IS THE FIRST INSTRUCTION ANYBODY HAS GIVEN THE BOY ON THAT WALL IN SIX YEARS, AND IT COMES FROM A MAN WHO CANNOT MAKE HIM DO ANYTHING.** The boy does it with a scraper and a bucket and hot water out of a bin room and does not ask why, because he has already said he is not spending a second question inside a month. **WHAT IS UNDER THE PAINT IS SIXTY-TWO OF THE MONTH'S FORMS, STACKED IN THE WALL, ONE A MONTH SINCE THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER OF THE FIRST YEAR, EVERY ONE OF THEM WITH TWO EMPTY SQUARES AT THE FOOT, PAINTED INTO.** About two hundred people a day have stopped at that corner for six years and read the top half of the top one. Below the lowest form is **four inches of dressed stone, and the two words are cut into it and go all the way through, and the left-hand one is deeper than the right-hand one because of the way a man works when he is cutting and not when he is writing**, and a notice is cut above them in the same hand saying where they came from, **and not one question has been asked of anybody in six years about who cut them and when and at whose asking.** **THE BOY MAKES A RUBBING ON THE BACK OF A MONTH'S FORM WITH A LUMP OF CHALK AND IT IS THE FIRST ONE THAT HAS EVER BEEN MADE.** Lin Hao says he will put a copy on the rail, **and the boy says no before he finishes the sentence, and gives the reason himself: a rail is a place a thing is read once by about nine people a week, and a corner is a place a thing is said — and a rail is the opposite of a reason.** He is right, and the sheet goes into the inside of a coat instead, and there is not one instrument in this city that will take a sentence off a person's mouth once two people have said it to each other. By the light going there are about nine sheets at that corner and nobody told anybody to copy it, **and a sentence is said out loud in the open there and gets it wrong about forty times inside an hour and is corrected by people who cannot write, which is the most correct thing anybody has done to his sentences in six years and it is going to be a different sentence by the spring.** Ends: **making good does not touch stone**, and the boy will be painting that corner in the spring because the render is his and the stone never has been, and there is nothing on the form he works from about four inches of dressed stone. |
| **649** | 27 Dec, y6 · 2,126 · 2,126 − 2,121 = **5** | **A Department That Answers Elsewhere** | **3,038** | The department of works, nine streets from the wall, and about two hours behind a barrow of broken paving again, because it is the twenty-seventh of December and a street somewhere else in this city is being opened up. **THE FORM GOES IN AT FOUR AND HE GETS A SLIP WITH A NUMBER ON IT, THE FIRST PIECE OF PAPER WITH AN INSTRUMENT BEHIND IT HE HAS BEEN GIVEN IN ABOUT A YEAR**, and the man at the counter says a number is not a way of getting anything and is a way of being found. **HE ASKS ABOUT THE TWO WORDS AND THE MAN AT THE COUNTER ASKS HIM WHAT THEY ARE — THE FIRST PERSON IN THIS CITY TO ASK — AND HE SAYS HE WILL NOT READ THEM ALOUD**, because the last time he did, about nine hundred people turned them into a count of something. The schedule is up in a corridor off the second floor and anybody may look at it: about nine hundred lines, an address, a line of work, a box for the date the work was done, **and not one column about what is on a wall, and there never has been, in thirty years of forms.** The line for this wall says *take down to course and make good*. **THE DEPARTMENT THAT ANSWERS ELSEWHERE: the wall is the works', and the painting is a Bureau contract on a schedule in a different building nine streets the other way, and the two offices have not sent a paper to each other in the nine years the man has been on that counter, and there is nothing that makes them, and that is not a complaint. That is a schedule.** At the painting counter: **making good is render, we do render, not one person in nine years has asked us about the foot of the wall, and a question is a question and not a return.** **AND THE LAST THING HE LEARNS, STANDING IN A STREET WITH IT IN HIS HAND, IS THAT HE HAS BEEN CARRYING THE ANSWER** — because the box at the foot is for the date the work was done and there is no way on earth to write a date that has not happened yet, and he has been carrying it for three hundred and six days without ever turning it over. Ends: three days to the thirtieth, a wall, a form, a coat with a rubbing in it, and a slip the size of a thumb-nail. |
| **650** | 30 Dec, y6 · 2,129 · 2,129 − 2,126 = **3** · **QUARTER-END, twenty-fourth sheet** | **The Wall Comes Down In The Spring** | **3,387** | **THE PAGE WENT OUT AT FOUR.** Eight hundred and fifty-two at the head unmoved across eight quarters, about four hundred and nineteen lines with a date and an hour against every hour, two lines in a column, four sentences at the foot, **six lines under those with a strike through the fourth of them and a name under it and two inches of nothing**, and a bundle of twenty-four going into a room nobody is obliged to open, one of which is an empty sheet of paper that came nine miles through a front door. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE A WEEK WILL GET TO THE BOTTOM OF IT.** The corner at about ten past four. **THE SIXTY-THIRD FORM GOES UP AND IT IS THE LAST ONE THAT WILL GO UP ON THAT CORNER WHILE THERE IS A CORNER**, because the render comes off in the spring and the wall goes down to the course and what will be left is four inches of dressed stone and about nine feet of new render and nothing to put a board against. About two hundred people a day stop and about two of them get to the foot. **Emil Brack reads the four sentences at the foot of the twenty-fourth page out loud at the wall end, in other words, and is right about every part of it and is not thanked; two people said thank you and neither of them said it to him.** **A sentence at that corner is wrong about forty times inside an hour and gets corrected, because a sentence is a thing you can argue with, and there is not one instrument in Ostara that will take any of it back down.** He stands nine feet off for the second time in about ninety days and **this time he came up the hill to do nothing at all.** Ends on the boy, who will not let him ask anything, who has been asked nothing three times, and who says that in the ninth month of next year he is going to put the sixty-fourth up on that corner with both squares empty, **and that there is not one instrument in this city that will put a figure in that box, and he is the man who paints it and he is not going to be able to put one in it either.** And the wall comes down in the spring, and it has not come down yet. |

| **631** | 8 Aug, y6 · 1,987 · 1,987 − 1,979 = **8** after 630 | **A Division That Reads Everything Twice** | **2,662** | **THE SEAM.** A division reads every paper twice — once by the officer holding it in a district, once at the desk — and puts the two readings into two books four feet apart and has never laid them together. **THE ROUND BOOK SAYS SIX HUNDRED AND NINETY-ONE, THE DESK BOOK SAYS SIX HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-FOUR, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS SEVENTEEN**, and thirteen of them can be named and four cannot, and not one of the seventeen was refused anybody: a counter three streets away, a person entered twice in one afternoon, a woman who came four times and put one thing down. **IT IS THE FIRST NUMBER IN THIS VOLUME NOBODY CAN CHECK.** Wren Kallo takes no question and says why: **a desk takes one question of a thing and only one and does not spend it on a person**, and she has had one since the spring. **Beatrix Halm carries ninety-one people a quarter who came to her counters and were entered nowhere, counted on her fingers since the second year, and will not write it down.** **THE CHANGE: HE IS TOLD THAT THE RULE WHICH DECIDES HOW MANY CLAIMS THIS OFFICE HAS PRODUCED IS NOT HIS AND IS OLDER THAN THE OFFICE**, which is the first thing anybody has said to him in six years that he did not already know. He wants a figure somebody would check. Ends: the twenty-third of September, fifty-two days off, and about nine people a week. |
| **632** | 15 Aug, y6 · 1,994 · 1,994 − 1,987 = **7** | **The Landlord's Man Is Right** | **2,827** | **A LANDLORD'S MAN, AND HE IS RIGHT ABOUT EVERY FACT INCLUDING THE ONE THAT HURTS HIM.** Four days standing about nine feet off a desk in a four-foot passage because he has understood that he is not to go in. **HE ASKS FOR THE FOURTH LINE TO BE PUT BACK ON A BOARD**, out loud, in a passage, without lowering his voice, and he is right: it is the only instrument in this city a landlord may put in front of a magistrate that cannot be answered, and it is why he lost a hearing on the eleventh in about thirteen minutes, and the first three lines went through a door in nine minutes because they could be checked. **THE FACT THAT COSTS HIM: about nine demands in this city carry a figure off a slate in a lane and one is his, and a man in a delivery coat has read the reason for every figure out loud at a wall end at ten to six since the thirteenth of July, and he has not gone because he is afraid of what he will be told.** He went on the seventeenth and got what he went for and it is worth nothing, **because every claim in this city now dies on the third line and the somebody in question is a landlord.** He asks Lin Hao to take it to a counter with the office's address at the head of it; there is no room at the head of a demand. **HE CAME TO BE REFUSED AND SAYS SO.** Want: somebody to tell him he could not do anything; it lasts eleven minutes. Ends: the sixth person this month to stand at that desk, and the first who wanted something that is not about the seventh of July. |
| **633** | 23 Aug, y6 · 2,002 · 2,002 − 1,994 = **8** · 633 − 623 = **10** · 633 − 118 = **515** · **SCENE** | **One Question On A Returns Desk** | **4,152** | **THE SCENE. WREN KALLO, 515 after 118, 10 after 623. ON THE RETURNS DESK IN THE GROUND FLOOR OF A DIVISION BUILDING AT HALF PAST FIVE, WITH NINE SHUT COUNTERS, A LAMP SHE WOULD NOT TURN OFF, AND FOUR FEET OF PASSAGE. NOT IN HER ROOMS.** **THE AXIS IS THE ONE QUESTION**, carried by the rule of a desk and named by nobody: a desk takes one question of a thing and only one, and it may not be put in a room the person owns. **THE QUESTION IS *IS A QUESTION A RETURN*, IT HAS BEEN IN HER HAND SINCE THE SPRING, AND SHE SPENDS IT HERE ON A PIECE OF PAPER ABOUT A COUNTER.** Three division officers' questions have been on that tray since the spring; a fourth paper with three lines and no address came on it at a quarter past four with nobody in the building. **He gives her the wrong answer in four seconds and she is entitled to the one she asked, and works out her own: a question is a return when somebody returns it, nobody has, and she is going to return them into a day book, which is not a return and goes to no magistrate.** She is direct, immodest and unhurried: **she says out loud that she has wanted him since the seventh of March and that there is no reason for it and she looked for one for a fortnight**, and that she will not be modest about any of it. Two nails of her left hand gone into ink, once, in her own words; the yellow is not used. **No violence, no restraint, no sedation, no exhaustion, no seal. Both adults; he is rank one with a spent chest and no permission.** Set B at word boundary: **0**, and 603 returns 1 on the same list, which is the parity check. Closes on a paper she cannot account for and on the fifteenth of September. |
| **634** | 1 Sep, y6 · 2,010 · 2,010 − 2,002 = **8** | **The Standing He Cannot Strike** | **2,421** | The rent board off Cordwain — a table, three chairs, a stove, a window shut and painted years ago. **CORVIN ASHBY, WHO IS NOT CAREFUL, AND WHO IS RIGHT.** The matter is listed for the fifteenth of September. **If she comes and does not understand it the case goes on without her; if she does not come it goes on without her; those are not two things but one thing with a door in it, and he has won two of these in nine years and did not have to describe a coat.** **THE BAR IS THAT NOT ONE WORD OF A DEMAND SAYS THE PERSON UNDERSTOOD IT** — and Ashby will go further than the question: if she were given every courtesy in the world, in a room with a door onto a street, with a person behind her told to speak slowly, **and it did not help her, then no bar in the world would touch him, and that is what a bar is; it does not depend on a fact.** **THE STANDING NOBODY CAN STRIKE IS A PROVED LANDLORD'S, THERE IS NO INSTRUMENT IN OSTARA THAT STRIKES ONE, AND THE MAN STANDING IN FRONT OF HIM IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THE CITY WHO WOULD WANT TO.** He says nothing. In the passage the landlord's man tells him the figure he went to the wall end for is worth nothing. **HE DECIDES TO BE IN THE ROOM ON THE FIFTEENTH WITH NO STANDING IN IT**, and Ashby tells him what that will look like and that it is the seventh of July again, and about nine hundred people will not be able to tell in nine years whether a hearing was about a shop rent or about the man who wrote four lines. Want: somebody to tell him he could not do anything. |
| **635** | 8 Sep, y6 · 2,017 · 2,017 − 2,010 = **7** | **Nineteen Years And No Name** | **2,601** | The bound ledger behind the returns desk is nineteen years old and lists every counter that has ever stood in that district, with a figure against each and **no name against any**. **NINE HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE against a counter that shut nineteen years ago and has not moved in all of that, because a counter figure can be added to and cannot be taken from** — a return has one column and it adds, and numbers in that building can only grow. **THE RULE IS NOT ANYBODY'S.** It is older than the Register, in no hand, in no book she has ever been shown. **He has spent six years believing that every instrument there is for not doing a thing was put there by somebody, mostly by him, and she tells him he did not write this one, and it is the first thing anybody has said to him in six years that he did not already know.** The change he cannot give her: **she is going to stop counting the ninety-one, and she gives the reason herself** — on the fifteenth somebody in a room will say there is nothing anybody can do about it, and after that a counter figure is the only kind of figure this city will accept, and one number in her own head becomes an argument somebody can make against her. **She is not brave and has never once been brave.** Nineteen years re-derived in the footer as six thousand eight hundred and forty days and minus four thousand eight hundred and twenty-three. Ends: the sixth person this month at that desk, and something that is going to happen on a morning. |
| **636** | 15 Sep, y6 · 2,024 · 2,024 − 2,017 = **7** | **Three Lines And A Yard** | **2,852** | The yard first. About eleven thousand people, about nine hundred who can read all of what is on the shutter, a queue of about four hundred people waiting to have three lines read to them, and a woman of about forty who came up at a quarter to ten to ask which of the three is about a person, is told, **and says she has been having the third one read to her since the twenty-third of July and that it is the one she has been needing** — and warns him in advance that if he says anything in the room she will stand up and say *that is a man who is not a party*. **Then the room.** About forty people. He stands at the back against a wall in a rank-one coat and nobody looks at him, and he wants somebody to ask him something more than he has wanted anything. **THE THIRD LINE DIES IN NINE MINUTES.** It is put at the ordinary speed, the woman brings the true answer with her and dressed for it — she cannot describe a man who came to her door at night for four minutes and did not give his name — and it is the end of it, and it is correct. **IN THE CORRIDOR SHE SAYS THE FIRST LINE OUT LOUD TWICE, and it is the first time in six years that a sentence of his has been said by the person it was written for**, and she starts on the third and he tells her not to, and tells her in a corridor with nine people in it the sentence he has told four rooms: the four were entered against himself on the fifteenth of October of the fourth year and a clerk entered one sentence saying the fourth was not met because the thing it describes is on a page in this city. The landlord's man says he saw him at the back and is not going to have told his employer. **Ends at four o'clock with a man on a ladder putting the third of the three lines on a fourth board in four-inch letters because it is the one that works.** |
| **637** | 22 Sep, y6 · 2,031 · 2,031 − 2,024 = **7** | **The Sentence In The Office's Hand** | **2,348** | **EMIL BRACK COMES UP THE STAIR BEHIND A LANE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THREE YEARS TO HAVE THE SENTENCE READ TO HIM.** He can read. He read the ninth line down off a desk under a window out of his own memory twice. **He needs the man who wrote it to say it out loud in a room with another person in it, and the reason he gives is that the sentence was never a warning to him — it is the only piece of paper in this city in which his name appears at all.** Wenna Raut spends her one question of the day, *were these the words you gave me*, and says the rest is not hers to do. **There is exactly one person in Ostara entitled to read it aloud and he is not permitted to, and no instrument will let anybody do it again.** He reads it in other words. Brack: **it is now a sentence two men have heard one other man say out loud, and there is no version of a sentence a person has heard spoken that is still a warning to anybody.** He asks for it to be taken off and is refused, four reasons deep — a clerk may only enter what she is given, nothing in this city will take a line off that page in the office's hand, it is not his to strike, and if he strikes it in his own hand he puts his own handwriting at the end of a sentence he admitted inventing. **Then he says the sentence this chapter exists for: *then it stays because it is in your hand and not because it is right*, and Lin Hao has to answer *it stays because it is in your hand*, and Brack says he is right about it for the rest of his life and will never be able to be told he is not.** Brack is thanked, diminished, defeated and wrong about no fact. He goes down **one** flight, not two, and says he will not be at the foot of that board on the twenty-fourth, on purpose. |
| **638** | 30 Sep, y6 · 2,039 · 2,039 − 2,031 = **8** · **QUARTER-END, twenty-third sheet** | **The Twenty-Third Sheet** | **3,410** | **THE OFFICE'S AGE IS PRINTED HERE AND ONLY HERE IN 631–640: 2,039 = 30 × 67 + 29, sixty-seven months and twenty-nine days.** The thirtieth is the last working day of month and quarter and the sheet goes out at four for the twenty-third time: the head unmoved across seven quarters, about four hundred and nineteen lines with an hour against every hour and no figure, the second column with two lines, four sentences at the foot. **THE BUNDLE HAS FIVE THINGS IN IT** and the fifth is a letter asking the office to correct a number, which came in through the front door because a board on a hill carries this office's address at the top of it in a hand anybody can read. At half past four the man at the desk says seven, and says the traffic at the rail has changed for the first time in nine years. **THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS HIS.** Somebody has chalked eight hundred and fifty-two on a board at the foot of a shutter, and the woman of about sixty who put it up has been telling nine hundred people for six days that it is how many people there are owed something, and her husband got it off the rail. **HE GOES UP THE HILL AT FOUR AND SAYS WHAT IT IS, TWICE, AT THE SAME SPEED, WITH NOTHING IMPROVED. ABOUT NINE HUNDRED HEAR IT AND ABOUT TWO HUNDRED WRITE IT ON THEIR HANDS. IN ABOUT ELEVEN MINUTES THE YARD HAS MADE IT A COUNT OF CLAIMS, and a man with a rag takes down the six-inch sentence about a thing nobody can prove, correctly, because a sentence is a thing you can argue with and a figure is a thing you can only count. HE STANDS NINE FEET OFF AND LETS IT HAPPEN.** What it closes and what it costs are at `state/current.md` §0z. |
| **639** | 12 Oct, y6 · 2,051 · 2,051 − 2,039 = **12** · **THE LONGEST INTERVAL IN THE MOVEMENT** | **A Form That Says The Spring** | **2,490** | **TWELVE DAYS, AND NOT ONE OF THOSE TWELVE DAYS IS NAMED IN THIS CHAPTER.** The ranks wall. A boy of sixteen with a paste brush puts the month's form up on the last working day of the month; **two ruled boxes at the foot, one for the figure of the thing at the address and one for the standing of it**, and about two hundred people a day stop at that corner and about two of them get to the bottom. **THE BOY ASKS, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN SIX YEARS, WHAT THE TWO WORDS ARE FOR.** He is told — where the Register has nothing, it has not been given anything — and works out in about four seconds that the two words say the same thing in different words, and that he has known it since he was about eleven, and that he therefore has nothing to do. **THE FORM FROM THE DEPARTMENT OF WORKS HAS BEEN IN HIS COAT FOR TWO HUNDRED AND THIRTY-ONE DAYS AND HE HAS DONE NOTHING WITH IT**, and the boy says there is no schedule in this city for who paints a wall and that he is not going to be painting in the spring anyway, because making good is not painting and nobody has ever given him a form for it — **and that his father would have taken the form in and it would have gone on a shelf with about nine hundred others, and that is what would have happened, and we would have felt better.** Emil Brack is at the wall end with about nine people because a wall is where people stand, and has been every morning since the thirteenth of July and has told nobody. Wren Kallo: **the twenty-third sheet went out at four with nine copies and three questions went out in the same four minutes in a day book in a division building and went to nobody.** He goes back down and there is a board at the corner with a figure on it and the word CLAIMS under it, and a clean rectangle in the dust where the yard's own sentence stood for six weeks, and he says one word to nobody about any of it. |
| **640** | 21 Oct, y6 · 2,060 · 2,060 − 2,051 = **9** | **The Figure Box Is Blank** | **2,412** | **THE MOVEMENT CLOSES ON A SENTENCE THAT IS TRUE, IS NOT A COMFORT, AND HAS NO INSTRUMENT BEHIND IT.** The left box is for the figure of the thing at the address, and the address on the form is this office's, and the thing at the address of this office is a page and not a wall. **HE COULD PUT EIGHT HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO IN THAT BOX AND ABOUT TWO HUNDRED PEOPLE A DAY WOULD STOP AT IT AND ABOUT TWO WOULD GET TO THE BOTTOM AND IN NINE DAYS THERE WOULD BE A FIGURE IN THE OFFICE'S HAND AT THE BOTTOM OF A WALL. HE MAKES THE DECISION IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND LEAVES IT BLANK**, and the boy says he is going to be asked again by somebody who is not him, and names her. **She comes on the twenty-third and asks for a retraction, not a figure** — she put eight hundred and fifty-two on a board on the twenty-fourth of September and has told about two hundred people what it means — **and then says out loud that she is not going to give it**, because in about nine days it is the only thing she has to stand in front of nine hundred people with, and because she cannot write her own name and the page at the foot of which there are four sentences is the only thing she has ever read to the bottom of. **AND AT HALF PAST FOUR A MAN WITH A PENCIL AND A LEDGER OF ABOUT THIRTY NAMES HAS BEEN PUTTING DATES IN IT SINCE THE END OF SEPTEMBER BECAUSE SOMETHING CHANGED IN THE MIDDLE OF THE YEAR, AND A WOMAN LEFT A PIECE OF PAPER ABOUT A FIGURE TWO INCHES AWAY FROM HIM AND HE PICKED IT UP AND WROTE THE DATE ON IT IN PENCIL BECAUSE HE HAS A PENCIL AND A LEDGER AND NO FORM ABOUT ANYTHING.** That is the whole of what this city has got. |

She proves it out of a second book of her own, not the guild's and unsealed, with an entry written off in the fourth year and his fourth sentence entered beside it in her own hand, a book she has had in that room since long before there was anything in it, never shown to anybody. **The entry has stood in the chained book since the sixteenth of September, the anchor 607 has already printed and the only one this volume prints, and no year is given for it.** **The entry is written off on the second of May, in seventeen days, and there is no page in this city between this afternoon and then, because the office prints on the thirtieth of a month and the thirtieth of March has gone and the thirtieth of June has not.** She will not give him the carrier's address and the reason is not caution: if he does anything about it then what happens to her will be a thing a man in a building with no street door did to her, **and that is the fourth one, and there is nobody left who can be given any of them.** |

All nine of them, this morning. **There are twelve books and there were eleven and he does not let anybody read them, and he reads all twelve to her himself, in the order they were filled, with the date and the weather and the figure and the reason in plain words in the margin against every one he took on a hunch, and he does not stop and does not skip one.** Three or four of the queue stop. Which of them was me: **none of them has ever been her**, and there is no person in the margin against any figure he took on a hunch. And then the thing this volume is about, in her own mouth: **nobody told me.** Then the question of the volume — is there any way in this city of knowing whether a thing written down is true — and he answers it correctly: **no; there is a way of knowing whether a figure was put there on purpose by the man who wrote it, and for three years that was the only thing anybody had, and the office took it away in an afternoon in November on purpose and he did not ask.** **Lin Hao is at the wrong end of that lane for the whole hour and does not take one step and does not say one word, and Emil Brack does not once look down the lane for the rest of the morning.** She asks him to read the ninth line down and he reads it twice out of his own memory, from a desk under a window, with nothing improved. **She is not thanked by anybody and she is given nothing at all — no figure, no name, no column, no page with a column on it, and no answer to the question she came down the lane with. It is not a scene. NO CHAPTER OF 601–650 REOPENS IT AFTER 617.** |
| **618** | 29 Apr, y6 · 1,888 · 1,888 − 1,881 = **7** | **The Man Entitled To Read It** | **2,182** | The finding is listed to be read and the file is read back to the party first, which nobody in that court has ever had cause to do. **He wanted to be asked, says it to himself in a corridor at ten past ten, and is not. He is read to.** In order: the demand, the four lines, where they were sworn to, the office's answer of *it holds nothing*, nine division returns with the ninth one read in a woman's own hand, and then **THE FOUR SENTENCES AT THE FOOT, ALOUD, IN ORDER, BY THE MAN ENTITLED TO READ THEM** — the two that have been there since the office opened and get no air, then *A figure in Ostara may be a figure somebody made up on purpose. This standing sheet is printed by the office itself. Emil Brack.*, then the fourth. **This is the one verbatim printing of that sentence in 611–620 and it may not stand again in this volume.** The clerk may not ask a second question and Lin Hao may not ask one either and that cuts both ways, and the clerk says the three things he is permitted to say anyway: the fourth line cannot be tried by anybody in this building or this city because to try it you must know every page in the city; and a read-back is the one occasion on which a man entitled to speak gets to speak for as long as the file lasts. **THE CHANGE IS NOT LIN HAO'S. THE CLERK WRITES A MINUTE** — four sentences: that the fourth of four lines is a statement about the absence of a page, that the party produced nothing and cannot produce anything, that the first three can be tried and this one cannot, and that the rule relied on cannot be tried by the court or by any party. A minute is the only page in that file nobody wrote but him and it goes in front of a magistrate in about ten minutes and **there is no instrument in this city that will take it out.** He reads it back; Lin Hao reads it back again at the same speed, puts the page down, says it is correct and goes out, and the clerk does not look up, which is the correct thing to do and is also a kind of accuracy. |
| **619** | 6 May, y6 · 1,895 · 1,895 − 1,888 = **7** | **A Piece Of Paper Came Back** | **2,089** | **The four lines have become a thing people copy.** A woman in a wet coat comes into a branch corridor at eleven minutes past eleven, which is not an hour anybody comes to that counter, puts a demand down, says *this wants taking*, and goes. Four lines, three things written on them, dated the ninth of April, **and no address at the head of it**, because the landlord who copied them off a board in a division corridor left off the notice above the four lines. **He is right about the flat and has never heard of the office.** She comes back at half past twelve because a man at a rent board told her any paper carrying those four lines goes to the address at the head of it, **and there is not one, and the last counter open in this city at eleven at night is this one**, and she carried it here by accident. Petra Ilyin, once, without looking up: the page that would say it is not in that piece of paper. Nadia Ferrous closes it — **a field officer carried the January one up a stair by hand and wrote down that she had done it, and once is a thing that happened and twice is a woman walking a district on purpose to find a building with no street door.** He says he has been waiting since the seventh of February for somebody to ask him one question and says it will not happen; she says she has known since about eleven o'clock and is not going to make it easy, and tells him as a person who works nights and reads a page in nine seconds, and he tells her it is true and not a comfort, **and she is not going to say anything about either sentence again.** The sheet goes into a drawer at the back of a branch counter at four and it is still there in the morning. |
| **620** | 13 May, y6 · 1,902 · 1,902 − 1,895 = **7** · **THE MOVEMENT'S TURN, AND IT IS NOT HIS** | **The Week The Yard Decides** | **2,016** | He wants somebody to ask him what he thinks about a minute in a file he has read four times on a night shift. **Runa Sato comes in at twenty past eleven, stops three feet inside the door, gives him the first thing — that he asked for all of it on the eleventh of March by giving a man with no standing his name — and then the other thing, which is the reason she came.** **On the ninth of April a vestry booked its hall for one week, beginning on the twenty-first of June and running to the twenty-eighth, on an ordinary booking form filled in by a householder who has never heard of the Register.** About eleven thousand people are going to be in a room for a week and **it is the first instrument in this that was not put there by anybody in this building**, and he cannot put a word into it, cannot stop it, and cannot be told about it officially. The chaplain is not there and is not going to be asked. **She has come to tell him he is not going to be allowed in it, and not for his sake:** about nine hundred of the eleven thousand can read a minute as well as four lines, and if he is at the back of that hall then whatever is decided in it is a thing the yard did with the man standing in it, and in nine years nobody will be able to tell whether that week was about eleven thousand people or about one man who could not be got at. She will be in that hall every day of it on her round and will write nine lines and a tenth with a box, and the tenth is for a reason and a reason is not a conclusion. **THE CLOSE IS THE WHOLE OF THE MOVEMENT: SHE ASKED HIM FOR NOTHING** — not the minute, not a yard, not what he intends to do about any of it. He had spent six years being the only person an office is allowed to ask and five years being a man nobody could find, and a woman of rank two gave him the worst news he has had in about two months and took nothing for it. He stood in a corridor about eleven seconds away from crying for the first time in about two years. **He did not cry. He went up to the third floor with the bucket.** |

It is addressed to the office, which has never been named in anything and has no form on which a thing that is not a person may be made to answer. She can say she saw a man on the third floor at eleven at night; she cannot say he lives at an address. **There is no instrument in Ostara for serving a man with no standing**, so she serves it by hand, up two flights, and writes down that she did. She is rank two and he is rank one with no figure cut into his plate, and she has hated being above him for six years and finds this morning worse. She asks the one question she is entitled to — what is the office — and he tells her the truth in nine words and it is not a defence. **No clock inside the scene and no hour counted in it. Lin Hao has, for the first time in six years, been read. He says yes when asked whether the fourth line is still a rule if nobody can test it, and **about four hundred of the people who can read that board do not believe him.** |
| **607** | 14 Feb, y6 · 1,813 · 1,813 − 1,806 = **7** | **The Vant Cannot Close It** | **1,934** | He goes up to a counting house above a street off Cordwain to find out whether anything in the city holds four lines the way a book holds an entry. **Solveig Rask, keeper of the Vant's real books, twenty-six years in that trade**, gives him the procedure because procedure is not a figure: an entry closes when the thing it describes is paid or proved, and those are the only two doors. **Nothing in Ostara can prove an unrecorded deed, because proving one requires a page, and the fourth of the four says there is not one, and the office has printed that a figure may be made up — so a real book in this city cannot close that entry at all.** Cold anger at an institution for being right. **And then Rask tells him there is a sum put against that entry once a quarter, out of a book that is not that book, by a person four floors up who has never said so to anybody, and who does not know the entry was called at a valuation this afternoon because nobody is told.** She will not say her name, and the reason she gives is that the moment a person outside the trade does something about an entry in that book, the entry is read out and is then void. |
| **608** | 21 Feb, y6 · 1,820 · 1,820 − 1,813 = **7** | **A Form From The Department Of Works** | **1,946** | **THE WALL.** The boy on the ladder says the place that decides which wall is done in a quarter is nine streets away. He walks there, queues behind a barrow of broken paving for about two hours, and is handed a form: **the ranks wall, coming down in the spring, for making good.** One ruled box at the foot of it, for the date the work was done. A clerk turns the form round and reads it to him, which nobody in four divisions and two rent boards and one magistrate's counter has ever done. He drafts four lines, gets the fourth into the office's hand, and then stops in the middle of the room with his coat on: **the office may only enter what it is given, and there is not one piece of paper in this city on which a question travels to a department of works.** He has addressed a sentence to a piece of masonry. At eleven at night he turns the form over in his mind and understands that he has been looking at two blank boxes at the foot of a wall for about five years, and now he is holding a third one that will be blank before anybody writes in it and blank afterwards. **Neither four hundred is printed, derived or mentioned. Brack would not go and read it himself, because the whole of it would have been true and he would have been standing there doing it. **He tells Lin Hao, unprompted, that the office has made him legal** — he was a man who makes figures up, and now he is a man who does that, described — and that he does not know what to do about it and is not going to be told. **And that there is no longer any difference between his figures and anybody else's, because the office told the city that figures here get invented, which took away the only thing he had that nobody else had, which was that his figures were checkable.** That is not a compliment to him, a slate cannot be checked, and he said so before anybody printed it. **The lane's mornings printed once here with operands: 1,827 − (−118) + 1 = 1,946.** Lin Hao's feeling is envy so close to joy, and under it something colder: Brack is now the only person in Ostara who can say out loud what a figure is, and the office got there first, by three years, for nothing. **Brack is not thanked, not diminished, not defeated and not wrong about any fact.** Hook: he asks whether a second name can go under the first one, is told there is no form for it, and says he never expected one and never wanted one. |

The desk man reads him in six minutes and says the not-knowing is the answer, mentions a pencil in that drawer and a man who stood at it for about four minutes last October and put it back, and says he would not ask the woman at the stair either. **He goes up the two flights at ten past ten with nobody behind him and no hand on the rail. There is nobody at the top of them.** The door is shut, there is no sound behind it and no light under it and no chair back against it. **He does not knock, because a knock is a thing a man does when he wants something and he did not come up wanting something.** **WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of being the man who did not go up. **WHAT IT COSTS:** an hour of the night shift coming on at eleven, a morning he had not meant to spend, and the certainty that he could still be careful. He gets nothing, he knocks on nothing, and **no chapter of 611–650 goes up those two flights a second time.** |

## Volume 12 — The Surface

### Volume 12, Batch 0001 — Chapters 551–560. THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF DECEMBER OF YEAR FOUR TO THE THIRTIETH OF JANUARY OF YEAR FIVE. **21,947 WORDS ACROSS TEN FILES, COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND AFTER THE LAST EDIT. MOVEMENT ONE, PUBLISHED. THE FIRST BATCH OF VOLUME 12.**

**THE ONE FIGURE THIS BATCH FOUND WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS IN ITS OWN BRIEF, NOT IN A CHAPTER. `workspace/volume-12/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` §2 gave chapter 559 as spine day 1,433, and 1,433 is the twenty-fourth of January. The date is 21 January. Re-derived twice by methods that share no constant — `(y−2)×360 + 330 + (d−1)` = `3×360 + 330 + 20` = **1,430**, and `1,439 − (30 − 21)` = `1,439 − 9` = **1,430** — and the two intervals that depended on it were wrong with it: 558→559 is **5 days and not eight**, and 559→560 is **9 days and not six**. `1,430 − 1,240 = 190 = 6 × 30 + 10` days since the satchel, not 193. The chapters were not edited to fit the brief; the spine was re-derived and the figures went the other way.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, A WRITING PHASE FOR CHAPTERS 561–570, MOVEMENT TWO, *BORROWED*. VOLUME 12 IS NOT CLOSED. IT HAS FORTY CHAPTERS LEFT. `state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED.**

The second column was ruled on the ninth of March of year three = 758, and 1,406 − 758 = **648 = 360 + 270 + 18**, and it was empty on **seven quarter-ends** and has one line in it. **The first column has eight hundred and forty-one lines against it and about four hundred and nineteen of those are accounts, one with his name at the head of it and about four hundred and eighteen other people's words under it — and 841 and 419 are two numbers and are not merged.** **WENNA RAUT** reads the sheet twice at one speed and asks the one question she is permitted to ask, which is whether the four lines in the second column are the four lines he wrote, and he says yes. He does not open the drawer. **The sheet goes out at four on the thirtieth with the office's own sentence at the bottom, which he did not write and which is the only line on the page that says what the page is.** |
| **552** | 30 Dec, y4 · 1409 · QUARTER-END · 1409 − 1406 = **3** days after 551 · 1409 − 1334 = **75** days after 548 | **The Sixteenth Sheet** | 1,987 | **THE SHEET GOES OUT AND THE RELIEF IS WORSE THAN THE FEAR WAS.** *Register of Unrecorded Merit. Sixteenth quarter. Entered: eight hundred and forty-one.* **The figure has stood still through three quarters and a quarter is 30 + 30 + 30 = 90 days.** **THE TWO BLANK BOXES AT THE FOOT OF THE ORDINARY REPAINTING FORM ARE IN FRAME AND BOTH ARE BLANK, ONE FOR A FIGURE AND ONE FOR A STANDING, BLANK SINCE THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER OF YEAR ONE, AND HE HAS WALKED PAST THEM ABOUT SIXTY TIMES.** **About nine thousand people go under the ranks wall in a day and about two hundred of them stop and cannot finish the last line.** A woman at a public desk reads the whole page and says out loud that it is the address of the thing and not a count of anything, and she is right. **Then a board is up at the yard end with his four conditions in four-inch chalk and nothing at the top of them saying whose they are, and he stands at forty feet, tells a woman at a stall out loud that he will not come up that hill and say one word about it because of a sentence he wrote about that wall four years ago, and does not go over.** |

> In this volume, **Lin Hao must write the definition of an unrecorded deed for an office that has no instrument, no money and no column**, or **Ostara's second line gets a threshold under it that a man with a chalk chose, and the city's unmeasured half is settled in lanes**.

### Volume 11, Batch 0005 — Chapters 541–550. THE ELEVENTH OF JULY OF YEAR FOUR TO THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF OCTOBER OF YEAR FOUR. **22,641 WORDS ACROSS TEN FILES, COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND AFTER THE LAST EDIT (22,654 BEFORE THE 14 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR). MOVEMENT FIVE, CALLED. THE LAST BATCH OF VOLUME 11, AND VOLUME 11 IS COMPLETE AT 550.**

**THE 14 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR, AGAINST `logs/batch-0005.review.log`. FOUR FINDINGS, ALL FOUR APPLIED. NO CHAPTER RESTARTED, NO SCENE REPLACED, NO EVENT, DATE, CHARACTER OR TITLE MOVED, NO FIGURE CHANGED, NO PLOT CHANGED.** A wrong interval at 543 (twenty-five re-set to fifty-four) · a self-contradiction inside 548 (prose said she could never find out, notebook said she would find out from a page) · a fourth-wall break at 550 (*in this manuscript* re-set to *in this city*) · and a duplicated clause plus a truncated sentence at 546. **The words are 543 2,100 · 546 3,570 · 548 2,059, and the other seven files are unchanged.** Full record at the head of `state/open-threads.md`.

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-11/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, A CLOSE-AUDIT PHASE THAT WRITES NO CHAPTERS.** It was missing and has now been written: `workspace/volume-11/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`:148 told the writing phase not to create it, which contradicts `AGENTS.md` and `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`:153.

**THE VOLUME'S SENTENCE IS PAID, AND CHAPTER 550 SAYS SO ON THE PAGE IN HIS OWN NOTEBOOK RATHER THAN IN A REVIEW: the definition of an unrecorded deed was written for an office that still has no instrument and no money, and its column was empty for seven quarter-ends — **REPAIRED 14 OCTOBER 2026 FROM *FIVE QUARTERS*, WHICH WAS WRONG ON EIGHT LINES IN FIVE CHAPTERS. The column was ruled on the ninth of March of the third year = spine day 758, and eight hundred and forty-one lines sat in the column beside it; the count is taken from the ruling and not from the quarter in which Sun Meiling made her prediction, which was the other convention in Volume 11 and differed by two** — and has had exactly one line in it since the fifteenth of October, and the line is his own name. THE OTHER HALF DID NOT HAPPEN AND NOBODY IN THE BATCH SAYS IT DID — the second line at the foot of the ranks wall is still on the stone at the height of a name on a plate, a city cannot read it, and the man with the chalk is at the wall end of a lane at about a quarter to seven and has not been answered in two years. THE REACH OF THE RETURN IS NOT RESOLVED AND IS NOT ARGUED. `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED AND A CLERK STILL MAY ONLY ENTER. NO DAY OF THE WEEK, NO YEAR NUMBER, NO FLOOR, NO FIGURE OF THE ACCOUNT, NO ANNOUNCED AGE BUT AGNETE LINDQVIST'S CANON FORTY-FIVE. ZERO SYSTEM PANELS IN TEN CHAPTERS, WHICH MEANS FIVE BATCHES AND NONE SPENT AGAINST A VOLUME BUDGET OF FIVE. THE SECOND COLUMN IS FILLED ONCE, AT 548, BY HIM, AGAINST HIMSELF.**

| **511** | 8 Oct, y1 | The Registrar's Room | 2,168 | **THE ROOM, AND A YEAR SINCE THE WALL.** Two chairs, a table, a lamp with a taped flex, a window that does not open, a hook on the back of the door with nothing on it since February, and a pigeonhole under the table with two entries in it. **Eight months and eight days, thirty days in each of eight months and eight days of the ninth, and TWO PEOPLE AT THE DOOR IN EIGHT MONTHS** — him, and a woman with a slate on her own wall who wrote down *we cannot reach it* and put his name under it. **The room is lent out of a pool of eleven and at the end of the quarter the office must state in one line what it is for; Adeyemi has had the form in a drawer since the fourth of February and has not written it, and the line is eighty-two days off.** He says he will write it, and that it will be a sentence about what the office registers and not about what it is for, and that it will have a threshold in it — and he says that out loud in the room so that there is a witness to the moment he knew it. **He tells Adeyemi for the first time that nothing is under the white tile and that he does not know who put it there, and refuses an inquiry into the wall: a finding names somebody, and the only two candidates are him and a boy with a paste brush.** **THE LANE IS THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTY-SIX MORNINGS**, five days after the first anniversary of a stool put in a lane. |

| **501** | 1 Feb | Four Years Is Not A Number | 2,210 | **THE LETTER, AND IT SAYS THREE THINGS.** Eleven weeks and three days of a rota he asked for, a dry cistern, a standpipe, a woman with a bucket on the second floor who has been there every night since the eleventh of October and has never once asked him a question and has now told him that everybody who works that floor asks her for something inside a week. **A letter comes to the branch on the paper the Correction Office has, and it says the office exists, that Yusuf Adeyemi is its Registrar, and that Lin Hao is the only person the office may ask.** It is not a request and there is nothing to refuse it into, and the night man at the counter can enter the name at the top of it and will not, because a name at the top of a letter about an office would be an improvement. **HE IS NOT MADE REGISTRAR AND IS NOT OFFERED A TITLE.** At half past four two streets east Beatrix Halm says out loud what four years came to — two words and a dash and two more words a boy painted off a sheet somebody gave him, a wall about nine thousand people a day go under and do not read, a man in a delivery coat, and a slate — and he says that four years is not a number. **A white tile with his name on it and nothing under it. She says nothing is on it. He asks who put it there, and that is as far as it gets, in either direction.** |
| **502** | 9 Feb | The Man With The Slate | 2,510 | **HE IS NAMED: EMIL BRACK, AND HE IS DESCRIBED BEFORE HE SPEAKS.** A delivery coat with two fastenings gone, five stools of which two are his and one has sacking tied round the leg, a slate four feet by three, a figure of merit in four-inch letters, **a hundred and twenty-seven mornings derived on the page**. **He is right about every fact of it.** He counted the four days himself at the north end of the Reading with a piece of chalk, knows to this day that he lost about a hundred on the first morning, says so to everybody, and puts his own four days in small chalk at the bottom of the slate so that a person can check them against their own. He made the figure up in three days and has told everybody since and **has never once said the Board took his words off him and has been waiting four months for that wall to come and say it.** He takes a grain of merit a head off anybody who wants to stand in his line, which costs him eleven in the mouth and nothing at the end of a week, and has stopped looking for a way of doing it that costs anybody nothing because the looking was worse than the eleven. He carries for a chandler four days and does a stair on a fifth and is up at four because the lane is cold at half past nine. He carries Hanneke Ruis's and Beatrix Halm's four hundred next to his own and neither of them can see the other's sheet. **He is not defeated, not bought, not licensed, given no rank and given no plate, and he asks what the office is for and is told nobody knows.** |
| **503** | 18 Feb | Five Stools And A Lane | 2,469 | **THE DISAGREEMENT IS THE CHAPTER.** Beatrix Halm's rule: a round writes a line when there is something on it. Runa Sato's rule: a round writes a line when a district has decided something. **RUNA SATO'S FRAME IS THE CORRECT ONE AND IT IS HER OWN FROM THE SECOND OF OCTOBER: the people in the line are not behind him, they are in a lane, they happen to be behind a man with a stool.** What the district believed on the eighteenth: that it is a queue, four in five unprompted; that the man is running it, which is wrong and was arrived at from the stools; and that it will end, which every one of the nineteen said unprompted, and two of them gave a length. **SHE IS RIGHT AND IT DOES NOT HELP. A binding instrument is one at a time, so she cannot have one until the first is discharged, and only the holder can discharge it, and she cannot remember his face or his voice or whether he was in a room or a doorway, and **she has never had it in the first place.** **AND WHAT SHE WANTS IS NOT TO BE RELEASED: she wants a second promise to hold the first one down with, because a promise to give has a limit in it and she found the limit at about a minute and a half into giving eleven years back, and the only thing that would make it different is a promise that says *stop*, and she cannot swear one of those at herself.** **He offers to swear her one and she refuses it: a second instrument from a man who is not the holder is a present and not a chain, and the second she called it a chain it would have a man on the end of it.** She writes eleven years on a sheet off a docket roll in her own hand with her name at the foot — **a written promise binds nothing, which is the whole of what she wants** — and she has two answers for who it is addressed to and likes neither, and does it anyway, at about half past nine, and says read it after and do not correct anything. |
| 480 | 26 Aug | 2,429 | **THE HOUSEHOLD DECIDES FOR ITSELF.** He stands up at six o'clock, before anything else, and tells nine women about the chest — **that he has had it since the fifteenth of July, that something in it is spent and will not mend, that a cough has come with it that belongs to him until he is dead, and that he has told nobody in four months, and that it was cowardice and not manners.** Then he refuses to chair it and gives the reason: *a man who cannot take a cold minute has got no business asking nine women to take eleven*, and goes and sits by the window. **Beatrix Halm states the motion and then states that the household cannot be represented by him, because he wrote the clause himself in nine seconds in June.** Ines rules after four hours: a minute of that room cannot carry a household's resolution, there is no box for it, and **something goes: a person goes.** Hands, counted by Runa Sato in pencil on the back of a docket with her own name deliberately not on the front of it. **Seven hands. Agnete Lindqvist does not put hers up and says so before anybody asks: she will not put the Church's name beside a return, eleven hundred of her people are going to come to a square in October, and she would rather lose than pretend it is the same argument.** **EIGHT TO ONE. And then he casts the ninth vote and votes against them and explains why in four sentences — every one of those names is answerable at a counter for four years, and a second thing that is not an argument at all: he wrote thirty-eight words in a minute in June that put him in a corridor outside the door of every matter about his address, for ever.** Marguerite reads it out at half past nine in the form of a call. It is not tidy and not one of them makes it tidy. **Nadia comes down the stairs for the twenty-sixth, after the washing up, as she said she would, and tells him he has until the first of October to work out how to say it in front of nine women who have just watched him vote no on them.** |

**WHAT THE BATCH CHANGED, IN ONE LINE.** **A man who had told nobody anything for four years told nine women the whole of it in one room, and three of them had already been reaching into the thing he needs to empty — one of them for eleven years under a promise neither of them can name — and when he finally put it on a table he gave them the price first and went out on the landing, and they did it without him, and the option of doing it alone is closed to him for the rest of the book, and the household has decided without him and will go to the Council's door without him, because of a clause he wrote himself.**

## Volume 10 — Returned

> In this volume, **Lin Hao must decide who the reservoir belongs to and give it back while the Council watches**, or **Ostara keeps measuring the wrong thing for another twenty years**.

**WHAT THE BATCH CHANGED, IN ONE LINE.** **A man who has spent four years finding the instrument for every occasion was given one on the morning of the twenty-sixth, in front of three hundred people, and let it stay in his pocket so that a good man could ruin himself for a true sentence — and then, four days later, conceded in public that the thing he has been carrying was never his, which is the one sentence in nine volumes nobody in this book could have made him say.**

The fifth is new, and under it is the word the clerk wrote on the fifteenth, and **she has not been told what it means either, and she asks, and neither of them knows.** At about half past four the return goes out on the ordinary stream to **seven** desks, **and the seventh is a wing off a third court that sits outside the Wing, keeps no register of addresses, has had a shape since the first of January and has never been named — and the return says five, for the first time in twenty years.** The requisition of the twentieth of February is **sixty-one days** old, two months and one day and not three months, can be carried, has not been carried, and he has seventy-five days. **The batch ends with a gap in a line carrying her reason in it sitting in the same room as the figure of five that endangers her.** |

**WHAT THE BATCH CHANGED, IN ONE SENTENCE:** *a man who went eight volumes without asking anybody for anything asked eight women at his own
table on the fourteenth of April having said the price out loud first, and lodged his whole standing at a public post at ten minutes to
nine on the fifteenth, and thereby supplied the fifth that makes a requisition of sixty-one days capable of being carried for the first
time since the twenty-third of December — and the price of both was paid by nine women whose names are now documents, one of whom put a
two-finger gap in a line on purpose and then worked out from the gap that she has been the officer of her own front door since the
thirtieth of November.*

**THE BATCH'S TWO IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS ARE 408 AND 409.** 408 closed the option of reaching nine without handing eight women over, and
there was never another door. 409 wrote away his whole standing in his own hand at a public post and **is the reason a requisition he has
spent fifty-five days enjoying the safety of became capable of being carried** — and he did it himself, to himself, in a queue, with a pen,
in about eleven minutes. **Neither can be undone and after 409 there is no version in which he is a man nobody can answer for.**

**THE SCENE BUDGET FOR THE BATCH IS ONE SCENE AND IT IS 406, EIGHT CHAPTERS AFTER 398.** Gaps on both counts for the whole volume remain
8 / 11 / 11 / 10 / 8, the explicit count identical to the mixed one because `PC-44` keeps the only partner with no gift off the schedule.

**THE TWO OPEN ITEMS ARE UNCHANGED AND ARE NOT DESCRIBED AS CLOSED ANYWHERE.** The eighteen-chapter explicit-count breach between 380 and 398
is still in the book and Volume 9's own schedule closing is not a cure for it. `PC-28` is carried forward and not fixed, and no chapter of
401–412 reproduces any figure from `chapter-0359.md`'s section head.

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, A WRITING PHASE FOR CHAPTERS 413–422. NOT A PLANNING PHASE AND NOT A CLOSE
PHASE. THE SERIES IS NOT OVER AND IS PLANNED PAST 500; VOLUME 10 IS *RETURNED* AND THE ENDING IS CHAPTER 486.**

**THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE IS BATCH 0001'S OWN CLOSING CLAIM AND WAS TRUE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN. IT IS NOT THE LIVE PHASE. ON 14 OCTOBER
2026 THE LIVE PHASE BECAME `workspace/volume-12/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, A WRITING PHASE FOR CHAPTERS 551–560, AND THE HEAD OF

`state/open-threads.md:4236`, AND ALL THREE ARE INSIDE BLOCKS ALREADY MARKED SUPERSEDED OR RETAINED UNCHANGED — THEY WERE FOUND
AND LEFT ALONE.**

**THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0001 IS IN `logs/batch-0001.review.log`. FOURTEEN FINDINGS, TWELVE FIXED IN THE PROSE AND ONE FIXED IN THE GATE
AND ONE DELIBERATELY NOT ACTED ON WITH ITS REASON PRINTED. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO PLOT WAS MOVED, AND `outline/ending.md` IS
