### THE OPENING POSITION, INHERITED AND NOT RE-DERIVED

Plate **8,131** against a band floor of **8,146**, printed on the card that came down at six on the morning of the
thirtieth of March, fifteen under, out of band by twelve, tolerance three, **and the two words went up the ranks wall
in paint at about four the same afternoon after ninety days of nothing.** Ledger **142 lines, nine turned, the ninth
still himself, nothing spent in nine volumes.** File 4,112, one folio. Composition **seven** since nine forty-one on the
thirteenth of March. **The requisition is live and crossed into the quarter at four o'clock on the thirtieth of March
with everything written on it, and what crossed was the standing of the room, which is three.** **The Council had not
sat since the twenty-ninth of December.** **A guild broker's name is in the fourth line of a quarterly return in that
room's keeping.** **Mrs Imrie spent eleven minutes at a counter on the second floor of a building she has never been
inside in her life and cannot be asked anything.** **Lin Wei is in a room with no window on the third sub-level and
his medical return went to the Vestry clinic on the morning of the thirtieth of March.** **Wren Kallo's bar is
untested. Dagmar Oyelowo has no gift and two of her three things are broken. He has a floor, a date and a direction
and no means of getting there.**

### WHAT THE VOLUME IS ABOUT, IN ONE PARAGRAPH

**RANK NINE IS NOT A BAND.** A band is a figure and a figure is read off a card; **the Ninth Seal has no card, no
floor and no interval, which is why the top tile on the Board is black and has never held a name in living memory.**
To be held at nine a citizen **lodges their whole accumulated standing** into a consignment — one movement, in the
citizen's own hand, at a public post, in the ordinary way, with no instrument and no witness and no holder. **It
cannot be partial, cannot be topped up, cannot be drawn against and cannot be given back, and a lodged citizen is held
while it stands, and the Concil of the Nine is the standing custodian of all nine — which means five people in a room
can answer for a lodged man without taking anything off him, because they already hold it.** **There has never been a
tenth, and the reason is not a cap: nobody has ever been willing to hand their whole life to nine strangers.**

**And so the ascent is an act and not a climb.** He cannot climb on merit. He cannot climb on the reservoir, because
not one grain of it is scoreable and that is the engine of the whole plot. **The only thing that can be given is a
figure other people have already put under him, and every one of those names is now a document somebody else can
produce.** **On the fourteenth of April he asks eight women at his own table, in one room, having said the price out
loud first. On the fifteenth he lodges it at a public post, and a consignment is entered in the room's book on the day
it is lodged and is not entered out on any day — and the room stood at three for twenty-six weekly returns and stood
at four from the first of April, so the morning he lodges is the morning the requisition that could not be carried for
fifty-five days — laid on the twentieth of February, and (30 − 20) + 30 + 15 = 55 — becomes capable of being carried.
He built the fifth himself. That is the batch's irreversible act and
it costs him the only protection he had.**

### THE SCENES, AND THE ARITHMETIC ON BOTH COUNTS

`outline/series.md` fixes them: **406 (Petra Ilyin) · 417 (Nadia Ferrous) · 428 (Runa Sato) · 438 (Beatrix Halm) ·
446 (Ines Duarte)**. Gaps from 398: **8, 11, 11, 10, 8. The mixed count and the explicit count are the same five
figures**, because `PC-44` puts Dagmar Oyelowo — the only partner in the book with no gift — off this volume's
schedule entirely and no scene is scheduled for a woman who cannot be given one. **The explicit count closes. That is
the whole of the obligation Volume 9 inherited from the eighteen-chapter gap between Chapter 380 and Chapter 398,
which is still in the book and which no state file may describe as closed, decided, cured, resolved or discharged.**

### THE WHAT THIS VOLUME MUST NOT DO REGISTER, IN ONE LINE

**It does not open Lin Wei's file, does not ask Wren Kallo about the address, does not give Dagmar Oyelowo a gift,
does not put a brother in Sun Meiling's mouth, does not print a band floor it has not read off a card in the chapter
that prints it, does not name a day of the week, does not announce an age, does not carry or receive or decide the
requisition, does not make an offer of any kind, and does not introduce a final enemy.**

### THREE FIGURES IN THIS ENTRY WERE WRONG UNTIL THE 13 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR, AND ARE CORRECTED HERE

**1. THE NINE ON THE CHAPTER 402 ROLL ARE NAMED, NOT COUNTED, AND THREE OF THEM ARE NOT AT FERRIER STREET.**
`bible/characters.md` fixes the standing household as nine of the twelve, with **Wren Kallo, Sun Meiling and Dagmar
Oyelowo** the three who are not sworn. **The nine are Ines Duarte, Beatrix Halm, Tessa Okonkwo, Nadia Ferrous, Runa
Sato, Marguerite Vale, Agnete Lindqvist, Petra Ilyin and Isolde Marchetti — and Tessa Okonkwo and Marguerite Vale were
missing from this entry's cast and from the plan's, which made the roll come to seven.** The mechanism that makes nine
women in three buildings one household is the bible's own and the plan had not invoked it: **the law binds the plate
and the bond, quarters are not the test, and a sworn member may hold her own plate and her own rooms.** So Ines Duarte
is on Lantern Row, Tessa Okonkwo and Marguerite Vale are on their own plates, **and only six of the nine live at
34-6-118.** Chapter 400 is the page that supports it: **eight women at that table, seven of them sleeping at the
address, the eighth a broker from the Vant.** **A writer who counts the kitchen gets seven and does not know why.**

**2. THE INTERVAL FROM THE ROLL TO THE LODGE IS THIRTEEN DAYS, NOT ELEVEN.** The roll is taken on the second of April
with 8,131 against the household's name, because a roll is taken on the day it is taken and the highest rank in the
household that morning is his. **He lodges on the fifteenth of April and is bound at nine: 15 − 2 = 13 (`PC-68`).**
`outline/series.md`:193 calls the Chapter 402 milestone *the standing household recognised at rank nine*; **that row is
a one-row shorthand for the milestone and is not an instruction to enter nine on the second of April.** The flag is
carried at §1b of `outline/volume-09.md`, on the 402 card, in the `PC-68` row and in the PROMPT.

**3. TWO TITLES IN THE BANK WERE RETITLED AND NEITHER MOVE WAS A PLOT CHANGE.** **414 was *One Hundred And
Seventy-One Lines*, which names a figure the ledger cannot reach by the end of the volume, and is now *The Ledger
Crossing A Number*. 438 was *The Roof Above The Reading*, which is geography this book does not have, and is now
*Nine Years At The North End*.** **And Beatrix Halm's last scene was printed as 349 and is 299** — Volume 6's fifth
and final scene is hers; 349 is a chapter title and not a scene — **which makes 438 the second scene she has had in
this book and leaves the gap arithmetic untouched.**

### Volume 9, Batch 0002 — Chapters 413–422. THE TWENTY-THIRD OF APRIL TO THE NINTH OF MAY. **WRITTEN. 21,524 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED.**

**THE TWELVE FILES ARE `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0413.md` … `chapter-0422.md`.** Batch 0001's own record is immediately above; the volume's opening position is in the block above that. `outline/volume-09.md` §6 MOVEMENT 2 HAS BEEN REWRITTEN AGAINST THE PAGE IN THE SAME COMMIT THAT WROTE THESE TWELVE, WITH **NINE NAMED PLACES THE PLAN WAS WRONG**, and `PC-69` to `PC-75` have been re-derived against the chapters and `PC-76` to `PC-82` are the batch's own additions in `state/continuity.md`.

**WHAT THE BATCH DID, IN ONE PARAGRAPH.** He spent the account for the first time in nine volumes, on a stranger, in a queue, at ten past nine on the morning of the twenty-third of April, and the count of turned lines went to ten, which is the first time in this book that it is two figures and the first time it has gone past a number he has never told anybody. He said that number out loud to Runa Sato at a Field counter on a street with a tram on it. A woman sang nine seconds in a corridor because a corridor is a road between rooms and there is a fence where the walls are that she has never seen, and something agreed somewhere within two hundred yards that she cannot name. Nadia Ferrous took him down four floors in her lift and priced her own bill out loud in a stopped car. **Eight women found out what the line they signed on the eighteenth of April actually is, which is that the seven of them are officers of the holding and the space in the middle of it is the only thing standing between all of them and a room of five — and Runa Sato did not go to the counter on the twenty-eighth of April, or on any date after it.** He found the wing off the third court and a woman who has been at its desk for nine years and who had never been told that the room at the end of the corridor stood at three. A clerk read a gap aloud into a minute and wrote the same word under it that she wrote under the fifth line on the fifteenth of April, and the chapter does not print it. A room of five entered the requisition as held, on its own initiative, with nobody asked, and the entry went out on the ordinary stream at four and Wren Kallo initialled it above her own part in four seconds.

**THE BATCH'S THREE IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS AND THE THREE WOMEN WHO PAY FOR THEM.**

1. **413. THE FIRST SPEND, ON A STRANGER, AND HE NEVER ASKED.** The stranger is a carter who works a lane two streets from his own front door and carried an old woman up three floors at two in the morning on the ninth of April because that was quicker than waiting for help. **WHAT IT CLOSED:** the option of never spending. Nothing he has done in nine volumes has ever cost a person he had not spoken to, and he had spent the whole of that protection on a man in a queue who had a slip in his good hand.
2. **416. A NINE-SECOND SONG IN A CORRIDOR, BY A WOMAN WHO WAS CARRYING IT SINCE THE THIRD OF JANUARY AND SET IT FOR THE FIRST OF APRIL.** **WHAT IT CLOSED:** the belief that her gift is local in the sense of a distance. It is a fence, and she has put it where the walls are, and a room within about two hundred yards has agreed with nine seconds that were not in it and she does not know which. It is not the song that went out in October and the chapter says so in one line so that the register item is not touched.
3. **418. RUNA SATO DID NOT GO ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH.** She decided to certify the composition of premises 34-6-118 on the twelfth of April at four in the morning, priced it in a loading bay on the twenty-seventh, refused to invoice it, and un-decided it at a kitchen table on the third of May because Nadia Ferrous said out loud, in front of eight people, that she is going to be the reason the paper says seven for the rest of her life. **THE CERTIFICATION HAS NOT HAPPENED. Runa Sato has told a Field office she is not attending and has given no reason, because a reason is a thing that gets filed.** What it closed: the option of a composition certificate at that address, in her hand, with her name at the head of it, before the quarter turns. **And the fourth, which is not a decision but is the sentence the batch ends on: at 422 he works out that he cannot ask that room for anything and that he can make it need something, and he decides on the ninth of May at about ten past five that he is never going to do that again, and tells nobody.**

**THE THREE WOMEN WHO PAY, AND NONE OF THEM IS THE MAN WHO ARRANGED IT.** A carter in a queue who was given back something owed since the ninth of April and never agreed to a single part of it · **Nadia Ferrous, who took that address from eight to seven at nine forty-one on the morning of the thirteenth of March, in nine minutes, on her own account, and has now said it out loud at a table she had kept it to herself in** · **Wren Kallo, who initialled a transmission above her own part in four seconds on a second sub-level because it was her desk and the rule was hers, and who was never asked, and who said in the same room that one of the two of them is going to keep not asking until one of them stops.**

**WHAT IS STILL TRUE AT 422 AND WAS NOT CHANGED BY ANY OF THE TWELVE.** Plate **8,131**, printed twice in the batch and only there, and **no band floor is printed anywhere in the twelve** · the room where nine people sit **stands at FIVE and is FIVE and the fifth is him** · a requisition laid on the twentieth of February is **CAPABLE OF BEING CARRIED and is not carried, not received and not decided, and it is seventy-nine days old** · **the first line anybody has ever entered about it is now in that room's keeping book and cannot be struck by anybody including the man it is about** · Sun Meiling **knows and has still not been told the rest**, and the chapter that changes that is not in this movement · **nothing is routed, nothing is classified, no folio is opened, no settle date exists** · **`PC-28` is carried forward and not fixed and no chapter of 413–422 reproduces any figure from that section head.**

**CHAPTER BY CHAPTER, AND WHAT EACH ONE ACTUALLY DID.**

- **413 (23 Apr). A QUEUE OFF THE THIRD COURT. 1,891 WORDS.** A public post, a queue eleven long, a counter that shuts at four whatever is in it. **THE FIRST DRAW OF THE VOLUME AND IT IS ON A STRANGER.** He does not decide to do it. The relief makes the man finished rather than strong, costs him four or five seconds of cold in his own palm, and the man says *you have got a cold hand* and nothing else and goes out into the street. **THE LEDGER SAYS ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINE AND TEN ARE TURNED AND THE TENTH HAS A FACE ON IT.** Ends on what a turned line is for: a door somebody will open one day in an ordinary way, with an ordinary reason, by somebody who is only being careful.
- **414 (25 Apr). THE LEDGER CROSSING A NUMBER. 1,953 WORDS. THE VOLUME'S FIRST SYSTEM PANEL, AND IT IS BECAUSE THE CHAPTER TURNS ON THE LEDGER.** `[Ledger. One hundred and fifty lines. Ten turned. It is not sorry about either of them.]` — one panel in these twelve and one in the whole volume so far, and the second is at 450. Two numbers, and he had been managing the wrong one for five years. **He says the number out loud at a Field counter at half past five with ninety people walking past it, and the woman refuses to write it down and then makes him say it twice.**
- **415 (27 Apr). THE STANDING ORDER SHE WILL KEEP. 1,980 WORDS.** A loading bay off Cordwain at twenty past five. **RUNA SATO PRICES HER OWN CERTIFICATION OUT LOUD AND REFUSES TO INVOICE IT**, folds the sheet once and puts it in her coat, and it goes home into a drawer, because a thing priced and not invoiced has not happened to anybody. **The standing order of the twenty-seventh and the note of the thirtieth are put side by side for the first time in six months and they are three days apart and they are not the same instrument.** The chapter's real discovery is a figure: **the composition she is going to sign is seven, and seven is a bill she did not pay and Nadia Ferrous did, alone, at a counter, in nine minutes, and there are eight people under that roof tonight.** She will not sign a figure that is a bill. She names the one other woman who could and says the name once.
- **416 (29 Apr). THE SINGER WHO CANNOT SING HERE. 2,193 WORDS.** The back corridor of the Bellhouse: nine worn steps, four doors, a fire door with a bar. **A CORRIDOR IS A ROAD BETWEEN ROOMS AND YOU CANNOT SING IN A ROAD.** She sums aloud because **nobody agrees with arithmetic — you check it**, which is why a kitchen table agrees with nothing. **THE SONG IS NINE SECONDS, CARRIED SINCE THE THIRD OF JANUARY, SET FOR THE FIRST OF APRIL, AND NOT DONE ON THE FIRST OF APRIL, because she has not been waiting for a day; she has been putting the day off every time the day got here.** She sings it. Nothing comes back up the stair. **And a room within about two hundred yards has agreed, and for nineteen years her gift has been called local, and local is a fence, and she has put it where the walls are.**
- **417 (1 May). THE SCENE. NADIA FERREROUS. 3,793 WORDS. SCENE BODY 2,054 ON THE RECORDED SPAN, 2,987 ON THE LITERAL SPAN, PROBE 24 AGAINST 30 AT 406 ON THE SAME LIST.** A lift between the Vestry arcade and the clinic level, four floors, about forty seconds, stopped in the middle of the shaft. **NOT IMPAIRED AND SHE SAYS SO FIRST, AND THE AXIS IS BROKEN AND NAMED ON THE PAGE: SHE GIVES NO TERMS AT ALL** — no list, no paper, nothing to write down, and he will have to work it out. **HER GIFT ARRIVES FIRST AND IS NOT THE MECHANISM: distance folds downward and the car is four inches narrower on each side by the time it stops.** She says out loud that she has wanted him since a stopped lift in the first year of knowing them and has never said it because he was always busy being careful at her. She goes up one floor at the end and it takes ninety seconds and costs her a day and a half and she told him that before anything happened. **HER ENDING BEAT IS HERS: the bill is not the address, which she gets back in four years. The bill is that the paper says seven and eight of us sleep under this roof.**
- **418 (3 May). NINE WOMEN AND A WITNESS LINE. 2,483 WORDS.** The kitchen at half past eight. **BEATRIX HALM, DEAF ON ONE SIDE SINCE THE VEIL AND UNABLE TO HEAR ANYBODY AT THAT TABLE, PUTS HER HAND FLAT ON THE WOOD AT TWENTY PAST NINE AND SAYS THE THING SHE HAS BEEN NOT SAYING FOR THIRTEEN DAYS** — a sheet went down off this address at four on the twentieth and has not come back. Runa Sato does the arithmetic nobody had done: two documents, two days, two reasons, quietly welded together since the thirteenth of March, **and the seven who signed are seven officers of the holding and the space is the only thing standing between all of them and a room of five.** Isolde does the other sum: the two sevens are the same figure and have nothing to do with each other. **Then Nadia Ferrous prices her bill at that table for the first time in her life, and Runa Sato does not go on the twenty-eighth, and the certification has not happened.** Beatrix writes two lines in one sitting for the first time in nine years and there is something under both of them.
- **419 (4 May). FOUR IN THE AFTERNOON, TO SEVEN DESKS. 1,734 WORDS.** He asks the only question a member of that room may put — *where does the sheet go* — and the clerk gives him all seven and says out loud that the seventh has never been named. **HE GOES AND FINDS IT AND THE WING IS NOT EMPTY.** A woman who has been at that desk for nine years tells him, unasked, that it is the first thing with an address at the top of it to go up that stair in twenty years. **A wing is not a division and nothing can be served there; it is on the stream because the stream goes through it.** The same run carries a weekly return she has never once turned over, with a figure about the room at the bottom of it, and it says five. She reads it at that counter for the first time in twenty years and says she has never been told the room stood at three.
- **420 (6 May). THE CLERK WHO READS A GAP ALOUD. 1,844 WORDS. `PC-72`'s CHAPTER.** The line is read into a minute twice, because that is what she does with an entry that carries names, and a gap in a line was read out loud by somebody in this city for the first time. **SHE IS NOT WRONG: A SPACE IS NOT A HOLE.** Nobody asks why, and the page names all four reasons it cannot be asked, including his own. **AND THE REVERSAL IS THAT THE BOX UNDER THE SPACE HAD BEEN BLANK FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND A CLERK FILLS A SPACE, SO SHE WRITES THE SAME WORD UNDER THE GAP THAT SHE WROTE UNDER THE FIFTH LINE ON THE FIFTEENTH, IN THE SAME HAND, AND SAYS IT ALOUD, AND THE CHAPTER DOES NOT PRINT IT.** Two of that word, two books, eleven hundred feet apart, and neither can be read by anybody including the two of them.
- **421 (7 May). THE WEEK IT COULD BE CARRIED. 1,710 WORDS.** The kitchen on the seventh and the arithmetic is done by a woman and nobody stands up. **Seventy-seven days since the twentieth of February, two months and seventeen days, not three months. Twenty-two days on which it could have been carried and was not. Fifty-three days left in the quarter.** She asks the room whether anybody wants it carried, nobody answers for eleven seconds, and she explains why the question was a waste of eleven seconds: eight women in a kitchen are not a room of five. **AND THE FINDING IS A CLOCK: A COUNCIL SITTING IS ENTERED A QUARTER AHEAD AND THE ENTRY FOR THIS QUARTER IS THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE, SO FOR FIFTY-THREE DAYS NOBODY IN THIS CITY CAN RESTORE A STANDING, TAKE ONE AWAY, OR LIFT A RECUSAL.**
- **422 (9 May). THE DAYS A SHEET WAITS. 1,943 WORDS.** Seventy-nine days in that keeping with nothing entered against it, and the clerk noticed on the sixth because of the address. **THE ROOM ACTS ON ITS OWN AND ON NOBODY'S REQUEST: ONE LINE, ENTERED, THAT THE SHEET OF THE TWENTIETH OF FEBRUARY IS IN THE KEEPING OF THIS ROOM AND IS NOT CARRIED. A CLERK MAY ONLY ENTER AND CANNOT CORRECT.** It goes out at four to seven desks, and the second is counter-forensics on the second sub-level, **where an officer initialed the transmission above her own part in four seconds, and the officer is Wren Kallo, and neither of them said one word about the address.** He stands at the end of forty feet of corridor and works out the only instrument he has: he cannot ask that room for anything and he can make it need something, and it always costs a woman who is never told she paid. **He decides he will never use it again and tells nobody, and it is the only thing in nine volumes he has ever decided not to do.**

**WHAT A LATER PHASE MUST NOT MISREAD FROM THIS BLOCK.** The seventy-five days in `outline/volume-09.md` is **the fifteenth of April to the thirtieth of June and it is correct on the fifteenth of April and wrong on the seventh of May**; 413–422 each derive their own interval and 421 prints fifty-three, twenty-two and seventy-seven. **THE LEDGER IS 149 ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF APRIL, 150 ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH AND 151 BY HALF PAST TEN THAT NIGHT, WITH TEN TURNED** — the volume outline's *about 152* for 414 was an inference from a rate and the page overtook it. **Runa Sato's certification of the composition HAS NOT HAPPENED AND IS NOW OWED AT 428.** **THE UNNAMED WING EXISTS, HAS A DESK, HAS A WOMAN WHO HAS BEEN THERE NINE YEARS, AND HAS STILL NEVER BEEN NAMED** — a phase that names it must coin the name on the page and collision-check it. **THE WORD UNDER THE FIFTH LINE HAS BEEN WRITTEN TWICE, IN TWO BOOKS, IN ONE HAND, AND IS STILL NOT PRINTED IN THIS REPOSITORY**; `PC-70` governs and a later chapter may read it and name it from the page and not from a state file.

## ARCHIVED ON 13 OCTOBER 2026 — VOLUMES 1 TO 8 — **NOT DELETED, MOVED.**

She refuses to use the office twice and says why: the day that book goes through that door it stops being a thing she did, and in about four years there will be a room with a man in a coat who can say *is there a page*, and there will be a page with a hundred and forty names on it, and somebody who cannot produce a deed will have not proved anything.
